Just that blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before these three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really. Like I was saying a minute ago, it's like that warm part of me is just completely gone. I used to really love watching those baking competition shows to unwind. My sister actually got me into them a few years ago. She bakes all the time—she even made this massive three-tier cake for my niece's sweet sixteen last month. It was crazy, it took her like three whole days to finish it. The frosting kept melting, though, because the AC in her apartment has been broken since July, and her landlord is completely useless. He keeps saying he's going to send a maintenance guy out, but it's always \"next Tuesday, next Tuesday.\" It's ridiculous how much rent she pays just to sweat in her own kitchen, honestly. She's thinking about breaking her lease. \n\nBut, um... yeah, anyway. I used to love putting those shows on. Now when I sit down on the couch with my husband to watch them, I know I should be enjoying it, or feeling cozy and happy to be home, but... I just don't. I don't feel anything good at all.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasurable or enjoyable, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I wish I did. But like I was saying, it's just... I feel like the warm part of me completely shut off. I get home, I sit with my husband, I try to watch my shows, and I know I should feel comfort, or even just a little happy to be off my feet, but there's just... nothing there. \n\nMy husband tries, you know? He brought home takeout from that Thai place we usually love on Friday. The one on 4th Street? They have the best Pad Thai, though the last time we went, they completely forgot the peanuts, which is like the best part. And the parking around there is just impossible now since they put in those new bike lanes. I don't even know who uses those bike lanes anyway, I never see anyone on them, but they took away half the street parking for it. We had to park like three blocks away in the rain once. \n\nBut yeah... he brought the food home, and we sat down, and I just... I couldn't even enjoy it. I didn't feel anything. Just empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since this emotional numbness started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have been. I mean, especially at work. Whenever I'm anywhere near receiving now, I'm just constantly checking everything. Like, I keep checking the racks, looking at the pallet height, scanning to see where the exits are, and I'm always looking over my shoulder to see who's moving behind me. It's like I can't trust the floor to be safe for even a minute. \n\nWhich is exhausting, honestly, because our receiving area is huge. It's the size of a football field. They actually expanded it two years ago when they bought out the lot next door. That used to be this old diner, Denny's or something, I can't remember, but they tore it down. It was kind of sad, actually, because my husband and I used to get breakfast there sometimes when we first started dating. They had these incredible blueberry pancakes. But the service was always so slow. One time we waited like forty-five minutes just for coffee, and the waitress was so rude about it when we finally asked. I get being short-staffed, but you don't have to take it out on the customers, you know? \n\nBut, um... yeah. I just feel like I'm always on edge now, constantly scanning the warehouse. I just can't relax.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I feel empty, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to feel normal again. \n\nMy cousin actually went through a really dark period a few years ago where she was talking like that. It was really scary for the whole family. She had just gone through this awful divorce. Her husband left her for his personal trainer, which is just such a cliché, right? And the trainer was like twenty-two or something ridiculous. They met at that gym on 5th Avenue, the one with the huge glass windows where everyone can see you working out while they're waiting for the bus. I never understood why people want to pay so much money just to sweat in front of traffic. I tried a membership there once, but they charge you for everything. Like, you even have to bring your own towel or they charge you three dollars to rent one. It's such a scam. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, she was in a really bad place, but she got help and she's doing much better now. She actually just started dating a guy who works in IT. But for me... no, I haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself or anything like that. I just want this empty feeling to go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest in most activities or a decrease in pleasure for most of the day, every day, since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's like, every single minute of every day. I still do my normal routine, you know? Like, I still get up and drive to the warehouse every morning. Though honestly, I might need to take my car into the shop soon because it's been making this awful rattling noise whenever I go over forty miles an hour. It's a Honda Civic, and usually, it runs great, but my brother-in-law borrowed it a few weeks ago to pick up some lumber for a deck he's building, and it hasn't sounded right since. \n\nHe's always doing stuff like that, too. Just borrowing things and bringing them back messed up. He borrowed my husband's power washer last spring and returned it without the nozzle. How do you even lose the nozzle? And when we asked him about it, he just acted like it was already missing when he got it, which is a total lie. My sister always covers for him, which drives me crazy. She's been doing that since we were in high school, always making excuses for guys who don't take responsibility. She dated this one guy in twelfth grade who used to make her pay for all their dates, even though he had a job at the movie theater and she was just babysitting. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Oh, about losing interest in things every day. Yeah, I don't know if it's all day, every day. It's mostly just that weird numb feeling I mentioned before when I'm at home trying to relax.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't enjoy the things you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about three weeks. Yeah, exactly three weeks, ever since the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker. It was just... a huge mess. The ambulance actually took forever to get to our loading dock because the driver went to the wrong entrance. They parked over by the administrative offices, which makes absolutely no sense because there's this massive sign that says 'Shipping and Receiving' with a bright red arrow pointing around the back of the building. \n\nBut people just ignore signs all the time. Like the stop sign at the end of my street. People just blow right through it every single day. Just yesterday, this guy in a silver pickup truck almost T-boned a mail truck because he didn't even tap his brakes. The mail carrier was so mad, he was laying on his horn and yelling out the window. I feel bad for mail carriers, honestly. They have to deal with so much, especially in the summer when it's boiling hot and those little square trucks don't even have air conditioning. My uncle used to work for the post office back in the nineties, and he said the heat in those things was just unbearable. He ended up quitting to open a hardware store, but that went out of business when Home Depot moved into town. It's a shame, really, because he used to give us a family discount on paint and stuff whenever we wanted to redecorate.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've lost any weight. I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes still fit the same. I usually get my work pants from this outlet mall a couple of towns over, and they're pretty sturdy. The last time I was there, though, the traffic on I-95 was just unbelievable. They've been doing construction on that one overpass for, I swear, three years now. \n\nAnd they always do the lane closures at the worst possible times, right in the middle of the day on a Saturday. We were sitting there bumper to bumper for an hour, and this lady in the lane next to me was putting on a full face of makeup while she was driving. Like, mascara, eyeliner, the whole thing. I don't know how people do that. If I tried that, I'd poke my eye out. My mom used to do that when she'd drive us to school, actually. She had this old Buick LeSabre with those velvet seats that smelled like vanilla air freshener and old coffee. She loved that car. She finally sold it to some kid down the street who painted it bright orange. It looks terrible now, like a giant pumpkin on wheels. \n\nBut, uh, no, my weight is about the same. I haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad, just... blank, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions. Which is kind of how I feel when I have to go to the grocery store on a Sunday afternoon. It's just a chore, you know? The aisles are always so packed, and the carts at my local store have those wonky wheels that squeak and pull to the left. I always seem to get the bad cart. \n\nAnd the last time I was there, they had rearranged the whole cereal aisle. Why do they do that? You get used to things being in one spot, and then suddenly the Cheerios are where the oatmeal used to be. It took me ten minutes just to find the brand I like. There was this lady stocking the shelves, and she looked so tired, like she'd been there since 4 AM. I totally get that look, working in a warehouse. You just hit a wall. But anyway, no, I wouldn't say I feel sad all day. Just that weird blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Um, no. Not depressed, really. Just that same empty feeling I was talking about. It's weird, it's kind of like... you know when you walk into a huge room and it's completely cleared out? Like the warehouse on a holiday weekend when the skeleton crew is on. It's so quiet you can actually hear those big overhead fluorescent lights buzzing. \n\nI hate that sound. They've been saying they're going to upgrade us to LEDs for, like, two years now. The maintenance guy, Gary, he says the parts are stuck on a cargo ship somewhere. Which is what everyone blames everything on these days, right? Everything is always stuck on a ship. I actually saw a documentary about those cargo ships on TV the other night. They are absolutely massive. The people who work on them have to be out at sea for months at a time, just surrounded by water. I could never do that. Just looking at the ocean makes me feel a little seasick. My parents took us on a cruise to the Bahamas when I was in middle school, and I spent the first three days just lying in the cabin eating saltines and watching old movies on this tiny little TV they had bolted to the wall. The cabin was so small, too, like a closet with bunk beds. My brother kept kicking the bottom of my mattress the whole time. \n\nBut anyway, what were we... oh, depressed. No, I don't think so. Just numb.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Interest in things? Um... I don't really know. I guess I haven't been doing much of anything lately outside of work. I used to go to this bowling league on Thursday nights with some people from the warehouse, but I just haven't felt like going. Bowling is getting so expensive anyway. The alley near my house just raised their prices for shoe rentals, and they don't even clean them properly. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. It's funny how places always ruin a good thing to save a few bucks. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. I tried complaining to the barista, but it wasn't her fault. She's this sweet college kid who's always studying for biology exams behind the counter. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. Like I said, it's really just that blank feeling. But speaking of \"a little bit,\" my supervisor at the warehouse, Dave, he's always walking around telling us to just give \"a little bit more\" on our pick rates. Like, how much more can we give? We're already scanning hundreds of boxes an hour. \n\nAnd those handheld scanners we use are practically ancient. The batteries die halfway through a shift, and you have to walk all the way across the concrete floor to the charging station. Which is right next to the breakroom. And I swear, the breakroom always smells like someone burnt popcorn. Why do people always burn popcorn in the microwave? It's like they hit the five-minute button and just walk away. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend. You'd think they'd put a sign up or something, but Dave says we don't have the budget for \"non-essential signage.\" Whatever that means. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, being depressed. Yeah, no, I don't think I'm depressed. Just that weird empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Low mood? No, not really. I mean, I'm not sad or down or anything like that. Just... blank. Like nothing's really registering. But speaking of registering, the new cash registers they put in at the grocery store down the street are so confusing. They replaced all the regular lanes with these giant self-checkout machines that talk to you. \n\nThe voice is so loud, too. \"Please place your item in the bagging area.\" Like, I did! I put the apples right there, and it still flashes that red light and makes you wait for the attendant. The attendant is usually this older lady, Brenda, I think her name tag says. She always looks so tired. I don't blame her, having to run back and forth swiping her card every time someone buys a cucumber. She told me last week her feet were killing her because she bought these new shoes online and they didn't fit right. Buying shoes online is such a gamble. You never know if the sizing is going to be weird, and then returning them is a huge hassle. You have to print the label, find a box, tape it all up, and then drive all the way to the post office. The line at our post office is always out the door, especially around the holidays. People shipping all these giant boxes to their grandkids... \n\nWait, what was the question? Low mood? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say my mood is low. Just... not really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood, even if it's just some of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed mood? No, really, I'm not depressed. Like I said, it's just this... nothingness. Just blank. \n\nBut you know what is depressing? The weather we've been having. It's been raining for, like, three days straight. My basement actually started leaking yesterday. Just a little puddle near the water heater, but still. I had to call my landlord, Mr. Henderson, and he's always so slow to fix things. Last year, the radiator in my bedroom was making this awful clanking noise, like someone hitting a pipe with a wrench. Clank. Clank. All night long. I called him four times before he finally sent someone out. \n\nAnd the guy he sent was this plumber who tracked mud all over my rug. It's this nice braided rug I found at a flea market a few summers ago. I love flea markets. You can find the most interesting stuff. I bought this old, vintage metal lunchbox there once—it had the Jetsons on it. I don't even use it for lunch, I just keep spare batteries and lightbulbs in it. Which is handy, because the lightbulb in my hallway keeps flickering. I swear there's a short in the wiring in that building. \n\nAnyway... no. Not depressed. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. It's funny how places always ruin a good thing to save a few bucks. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. I tried complaining to the barista, but it wasn't her fault. She's this sweet college kid who's always studying for biology exams behind the counter. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. Like I said, it's really just that blank feeling. But speaking of \"a little bit,\" my supervisor at the warehouse, Dave, he's always walking around telling us to just give \"a little bit more\" on our pick rates. Like, how much more can we give? We're already scanning hundreds of boxes an hour. \n\nAnd those handheld scanners we use are practically ancient. The batteries die halfway through a shift, and you have to walk all the way across the concrete floor to the charging station. Which is right next to the breakroom. And I swear, the breakroom always smells like someone burnt popcorn. Why do people always burn popcorn in the microwave? It's like they hit the five-minute button and just walk away. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend. You'd think they'd put a sign up or something, but Dave says we don't have the budget for \"non-essential signage.\" Whatever that means. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, being depressed. Yeah, no, I don't think I'm depressed. Just that weird empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Low mood? Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I know you keep asking about feeling down or depressed, but it's really just... I don't know, a flatline. Just blank. 

Speaking of flatlines, though, my car battery completely died on me last week. Just totally flat. I turned the key and nothing, not even a click. I had to call my neighbor, Greg, to give me a jump. Greg's a nice guy, but he talks so much. He stood there in the freezing cold for twenty minutes telling me about his son's baseball tournament. His son plays travel ball, and apparently, it costs a fortune. They have to buy all these matching uniforms, and they travel to different states every weekend. I can't imagine spending all your weekends sitting on cold aluminum bleachers eating stale sunflower seeds. 

I used to love sunflower seeds when I was a kid. The ranch-flavored ones. My brother and I would sit on the porch and see who could spit the shells the farthest into the yard. My mom hated it. She said it ruined the grass. She was so particular about her lawn. She used to edge the driveway with these little rusty shears every Saturday morning... 

But wait, what were you asking? Oh, feeling sad. No, definitely not sad. Just... yeah, just empty.

Just that blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before these three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really. Like I was saying a minute ago, it's like that warm part of me is just completely gone. I used to really love watching those baking competition shows to unwind. My sister actually got me into them a few years ago. She bakes all the time—she even made this massive three-tier cake for my niece's sweet sixteen last month. It was crazy, it took her like three whole days to finish it. The frosting kept melting, though, because the AC in her apartment has been broken since July, and her landlord is completely useless. He keeps saying he's going to send a maintenance guy out, but it's always \"next Tuesday, next Tuesday.\" It's ridiculous how much rent she pays just to sweat in her own kitchen, honestly. I wish I did. But like I was saying, it's just... I feel like the warm part of me completely shut off. I don't even know who uses those bike lanes anyway, I never see anyone on them, but they took away half the street parking for it. We had to park like three blocks away in the rain once. \n\nBut yeah... he brought the food home, and we sat down, and I just... I couldn't even enjoy it. I didn't feel anything. Just empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since this emotional numbness started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have been. I mean, especially at work. Whenever I'm anywhere near receiving now, I'm just constantly checking everything. It was kind of sad, actually, because my husband and I used to get breakfast there sometimes when we first started dating. They had these incredible blueberry pancakes. But the service was always so slow. One time we waited like forty-five minutes just for coffee, and the waitress was so rude about it when we finally asked. I get being short-staffed, but you don't have to take it out on the customers, you know? \n\nBut, um... yeah. I just feel like I'm always on edge now, constantly scanning the warehouse. I just can't relax.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I feel empty, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to feel normal again. \n\nMy cousin actually went through a really dark period a few years ago where she was talking like that. It was really scary for the whole family. She had just gone through this awful divorce. Her husband left her for his personal trainer, which is just such a cliché, right? And the trainer was like twenty-two or something ridiculous. They met at that gym on 5th Avenue, the one with the huge glass windows where everyone can see you working out while they're waiting for the bus. I never understood why people want to pay so much money just to sweat in front of traffic. I tried a membership there once, but they charge you for everything. Like, you even have to bring your own towel or they charge you three dollars to rent one. It's such a scam. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, she was in a really bad place, but she got help and she's doing much better now. She actually just started dating a guy who works in IT. But for me... no, I haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself or anything like that. I just want this empty feeling to go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest in most activities or a decrease in pleasure for most of the day, every day, since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's like, every single minute of every day. I still do my normal routine, you know? Like, I still get up and drive to the warehouse every morning. Though honestly, I might need to take my car into the shop soon because it's been making this awful rattling noise whenever I go over forty miles an hour. It's a Honda Civic, and usually, it runs great, but my brother-in-law borrowed it a few weeks ago to pick up some lumber for a deck he's building, and it hasn't sounded right since. \n\nHe's always doing stuff like that, too. Just borrowing things and bringing them back messed up. He borrowed my husband's power washer last spring and returned it without the nozzle. How do you even lose the nozzle? And when we asked him about it, he just acted like it was already missing when he got it, which is a total lie. My sister always covers for him, which drives me crazy. She's been doing that since we were in high school, always making excuses for guys who don't take responsibility. She dated this one guy in twelfth grade who used to make her pay for all their dates, even though he had a job at the movie theater and she was just babysitting. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Oh, about losing interest in things every day. Yeah, I don't know if it's all day, every day. It's mostly just that weird numb feeling I mentioned before when I'm at home trying to relax.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't enjoy the things you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about three weeks. Yeah, exactly three weeks, ever since the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker. It was just... a huge mess. The ambulance actually took forever to get to our loading dock because the driver went to the wrong entrance. They parked over by the administrative offices, which makes absolutely no sense because there's this massive sign that says 'Shipping and Receiving' with a bright red arrow pointing around the back of the building. \n\nBut people just ignore signs all the time. Like the stop sign at the end of my street. People just blow right through it every single day. Just yesterday, this guy in a silver pickup truck almost T-boned a mail truck because he didn't even tap his brakes. The mail carrier was so mad, he was laying on his horn and yelling out the window. I feel bad for mail carriers, honestly. They have to deal with so much, especially in the summer when it's boiling hot and those little square trucks don't even have air conditioning. My uncle used to work for the post office back in the nineties, and he said the heat in those things was just unbearable. He ended up quitting to open a hardware store, but that went out of business when Home Depot moved into town. It's a shame, really, because he used to give us a family discount on paint and stuff whenever we wanted to redecorate.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've lost any weight. I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes still fit the same. I usually get my work pants from this outlet mall a couple of towns over, and they're pretty sturdy. The last time I was there, though, the traffic on I-95 was just unbelievable. They've been doing construction on that one overpass for, I swear, three years now. \n\nAnd they always do the lane closures at the worst possible times, right in the middle of the day on a Saturday. We were sitting there bumper to bumper for an hour, and this lady in the lane next to me was putting on a full face of makeup while she was driving. Like, mascara, eyeliner, the whole thing. I don't know how people do that. If I tried that, I'd poke my eye out. My mom used to do that when she'd drive us to school, actually. She had this old Buick LeSabre with those velvet seats that smelled like vanilla air freshener and old coffee. She loved that car. She finally sold it to some kid down the street who painted it bright orange. It looks terrible now, like a giant pumpkin on wheels. \n\nBut, uh, no, my weight is about the same. I haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad, just... blank, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions. Which is kind of how I feel when I have to go to the grocery store on a Sunday afternoon. It's just a chore, you know? The aisles are always so packed, and the carts at my local store have those wonky wheels that squeak and pull to the left. I always seem to get the bad cart. \n\nAnd the last time I was there, they had rearranged the whole cereal aisle. Why do they do that? You get used to things being in one spot, and then suddenly the Cheerios are where the oatmeal used to be. It took me ten minutes just to find the brand I like. There was this lady stocking the shelves, and she looked so tired, like she'd been there since 4 AM. I totally get that look, working in a warehouse. You just hit a wall. But anyway, no, I wouldn't say I feel sad all day. Just that weird blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Um, no. Not depressed, really. Just that same empty feeling I was talking about. It's weird, it's kind of like... you know when you walk into a huge room and it's completely cleared out? Like the warehouse on a holiday weekend when the skeleton crew is on. It's so quiet you can actually hear those big overhead fluorescent lights buzzing. \n\nI hate that sound. They've been saying they're going to upgrade us to LEDs for, like, two years now. The maintenance guy, Gary, he says the parts are stuck on a cargo ship somewhere. Which is what everyone blames everything on these days, right? Everything is always stuck on a ship. I actually saw a documentary about those cargo ships on TV the other night. They are absolutely massive. The people who work on them have to be out at sea for months at a time, just surrounded by water. I could never do that. Just looking at the ocean makes me feel a little seasick. My parents took us on a cruise to the Bahamas when I was in middle school, and I spent the first three days just lying in the cabin eating saltines and watching old movies on this tiny little TV they had bolted to the wall. The cabin was so small, too, like a closet with bunk beds. My brother kept kicking the bottom of my mattress the whole time. \n\nBut anyway, what were we... oh, depressed. No, I don't think so. Just numb.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Interest in things? Um... I don't really know. I guess I haven't been doing much of anything lately outside of work. I used to go to this bowling league on Thursday nights with some people from the warehouse, but I just haven't felt like going. Bowling is getting so expensive anyway. The alley near my house just raised their prices for shoe rentals, and they don't even clean them properly. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. It's funny how places always ruin a good thing to save a few bucks. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. I tried complaining to the barista, but it wasn't her fault. She's this sweet college kid who's always studying for biology exams behind the counter. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. Like I said, it's really just that blank feeling. But speaking of \"a little bit,\" my supervisor at the warehouse, Dave, he's always walking around telling us to just give \"a little bit more\" on our pick rates. Like, how much more can we give? We're already scanning hundreds of boxes an hour. \n\nAnd those handheld scanners we use are practically ancient. The batteries die halfway through a shift, and you have to walk all the way across the concrete floor to the charging station. Which is right next to the breakroom. And I swear, the breakroom always smells like someone burnt popcorn. Why do people always burn popcorn in the microwave? It's like they hit the five-minute button and just walk away. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend. You'd think they'd put a sign up or something, but Dave says we don't have the budget for \"non-essential signage.\" Whatever that means. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, being depressed. Yeah, no, I don't think I'm depressed. Just that weird empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Low mood? No, not really. I mean, I'm not sad or down or anything like that. Just... blank. Like nothing's really registering. But speaking of registering, the new cash registers they put in at the grocery store down the street are so confusing. They replaced all the regular lanes with these giant self-checkout machines that talk to you. \n\nThe voice is so loud, too. \"Please place your item in the bagging area.\" Like, I did! I put the apples right there, and it still flashes that red light and makes you wait for the attendant. The attendant is usually this older lady, Brenda, I think her name tag says. She always looks so tired. I don't blame her, having to run back and forth swiping her card every time someone buys a cucumber. She told me last week her feet were killing her because she bought these new shoes online and they didn't fit right. Buying shoes online is such a gamble. You never know if the sizing is going to be weird, and then returning them is a huge hassle. You have to print the label, find a box, tape it all up, and then drive all the way to the post office. The line at our post office is always out the door, especially around the holidays. People shipping all these giant boxes to their grandkids... \n\nWait, what was the question? Low mood? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say my mood is low. Just... not really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood, even if it's just some of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed mood? No, really, I'm not depressed. Like I said, it's just this... nothingness. Just blank. \n\nBut you know what is depressing? The weather we've been having. It's been raining for, like, three days straight. My basement actually started leaking yesterday. Just a little puddle near the water heater, but still. I had to call my landlord, Mr. Henderson, and he's always so slow to fix things. Last year, the radiator in my bedroom was making this awful clanking noise, like someone hitting a pipe with a wrench. Clank. Clank. All night long. I called him four times before he finally sent someone out. \n\nAnd the guy he sent was this plumber who tracked mud all over my rug. It's this nice braided rug I found at a flea market a few summers ago. I love flea markets. You can find the most interesting stuff. I bought this old, vintage metal lunchbox there once—it had the Jetsons on it. I don't even use it for lunch, I just keep spare batteries and lightbulbs in it. Which is handy, because the lightbulb in my hallway keeps flickering. I swear there's a short in the wiring in that building. \n\nAnyway... no. Not depressed. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time, even if it's just in the back of your mind?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I know you keep asking about feeling down or depressed, but it's really just... I don't know, a flatline. Just blank. \n\nSpeaking of flatlines, though, my car battery completely died on me last week. Just totally flat. I turned the key and nothing, not even a click. I had to call my neighbor, Greg, to give me a jump. Greg's a nice guy, but he talks so much. He stood there in the freezing cold for twenty minutes telling me about his son's baseball tournament. His son plays travel ball, and apparently, it costs a fortune. They have to buy all these matching uniforms, and they travel to different states every weekend. I can't imagine spending all your weekends sitting on cold aluminum bleachers eating stale sunflower seeds. \n\nI used to love sunflower seeds when I was a kid. The ranch-flavored ones. My brother and I would sit on the porch and see who could spit the shells the farthest into the yard. My mom hated it. She said it ruined the grass. She was so particular about her lawn. She used to edge the driveway with these little rusty shears every Saturday morning... \n\nBut wait, what were you asking? Oh, feeling sad. No, definitely not sad. Just... yeah, just empty.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, yeah, actually. Honestly, that's what made me come in. I feel like the warm part of me just... shut off. Like, I sit with my husband after work, or I try to watch my shows, and I know I should feel comfort or even a little happy, but there's just nothing there.

The last time I was there, though, the traffic on I-95 was just unbelievable. They've been doing construction on that one overpass for, I swear, three years now. \n\nAnd they always do the lane closures at the worst possible times, right in the middle of the day on a Saturday. We were sitting there bumper to bumper for an hour, and this lady in the lane next to me was putting on a full face of makeup while she was driving. Like, mascara, eyeliner, the whole thing. I don't know how people do that. If I tried that, I'd poke my eye out. My mom used to do that when she'd drive us to school, actually. She had this old Buick LeSabre with those velvet seats that smelled like vanilla air freshener and old coffee. She loved that car. She finally sold it to some kid down the street who painted it bright orange. It looks terrible now, like a giant pumpkin on wheels. \n\nBut, uh, no, my weight is about the same. I haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad, just... blank, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions. Which is kind of how I feel when I have to go to the grocery store on a Sunday afternoon. It's just a chore, you know? The aisles are always so packed, and the carts at my local store have those wonky wheels that squeak and pull to the left. I always seem to get the bad cart. \n\nAnd the last time I was there, they had rearranged the whole cereal aisle. Why do they do that? You get used to things being in one spot, and then suddenly the Cheerios are where the oatmeal used to be. It took me ten minutes just to find the brand I like. There was this lady stocking the shelves, and she looked so tired, like she'd been there since 4 AM. I totally get that look, working in a warehouse. You just hit a wall. But anyway, no, I wouldn't say I feel sad all day. Just that weird blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Um, no. Not depressed, really. Just that same empty feeling I was talking about. It's weird, it's kind of like... you know when you walk into a huge room and it's completely cleared out? Like the warehouse on a holiday weekend when the skeleton crew is on. It's so quiet you can actually hear those big overhead fluorescent lights buzzing. \n\nI hate that sound. They've been saying they're going to upgrade us to LEDs for, like, two years now. The maintenance guy, Gary, he says the parts are stuck on a cargo ship somewhere. Which is what everyone blames everything on these days, right? Everything is always stuck on a ship. I actually saw a documentary about those cargo ships on TV the other night. They are absolutely massive. The people who work on them have to be out at sea for months at a time, just surrounded by water. I could never do that. Just looking at the ocean makes me feel a little seasick. My parents took us on a cruise to the Bahamas when I was in middle school, and I spent the first three days just lying in the cabin eating saltines and watching old movies on this tiny little TV they had bolted to the wall. The cabin was so small, too, like a closet with bunk beds. My brother kept kicking the bottom of my mattress the whole time. \n\nBut anyway, what were we... oh, depressed. No, I don't think so. Just numb.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Interest in things? Um... I don't really know. I guess I haven't been doing much of anything lately outside of work. I used to go to this bowling league on Thursday nights with some people from the warehouse, but I just haven't felt like going. Bowling is getting so expensive anyway. The alley near my house just raised their prices for shoe rentals, and they don't even clean them properly. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. It's funny how places always ruin a good thing to save a few bucks. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. I tried complaining to the barista, but it wasn't her fault. She's this sweet college kid who's always studying for biology exams behind the counter. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. Like I said, it's really just that blank feeling. But speaking of \"a little bit,\" my supervisor at the warehouse, Dave, he's always walking around telling us to just give \"a little bit more\" on our pick rates. Like, how much more can we give? We're already scanning hundreds of boxes an hour. \n\nAnd those handheld scanners we use are practically ancient. The batteries die halfway through a shift, and you have to walk all the way across the concrete floor to the charging station. Which is right next to the breakroom. And I swear, the breakroom always smells like someone burnt popcorn. Why do people always burn popcorn in the microwave? It's like they hit the five-minute button and just walk away. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend. You'd think they'd put a sign up or something, but Dave says we don't have the budget for \"non-essential signage.\" Whatever that means. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, being depressed. Yeah, no, I don't think I'm depressed. Just that weird empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Low mood? No, not really. I mean, I'm not sad or down or anything like that. Just... blank. Like nothing's really registering. But speaking of registering, the new cash registers they put in at the grocery store down the street are so confusing. They replaced all the regular lanes with these giant self-checkout machines that talk to you. \n\nThe voice is so loud, too. \"Please place your item in the bagging area.\" Like, I did! I put the apples right there, and it still flashes that red light and makes you wait for the attendant. The attendant is usually this older lady, Brenda, I think her name tag says. She always looks so tired. I don't blame her, having to run back and forth swiping her card every time someone buys a cucumber. She told me last week her feet were killing her because she bought these new shoes online and they didn't fit right. Buying shoes online is such a gamble. You never know if the sizing is going to be weird, and then returning them is a huge hassle. You have to print the label, find a box, tape it all up, and then drive all the way to the post office. The line at our post office is always out the door, especially around the holidays. People shipping all these giant boxes to their grandkids... \n\nWait, what was the question? Low mood? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say my mood is low. Just... not really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood, even if it's just some of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed mood? No, really, I'm not depressed. Like I said, it's just this... nothingness. Just blank. \n\nBut you know what is depressing? The weather we've been having. It's been raining for, like, three days straight. My basement actually started leaking yesterday. Just a little puddle near the water heater, but still. I had to call my landlord, Mr. Henderson, and he's always so slow to fix things. Last year, the radiator in my bedroom was making this awful clanking noise, like someone hitting a pipe with a wrench. Clank. Clank. All night long. I called him four times before he finally sent someone out. \n\nAnd the guy he sent was this plumber who tracked mud all over my rug. It's this nice braided rug I found at a flea market a few summers ago. I love flea markets. You can find the most interesting stuff. I bought this old, vintage metal lunchbox there once—it had the Jetsons on it. I don't even use it for lunch, I just keep spare batteries and lightbulbs in it. Which is handy, because the lightbulb in my hallway keeps flickering. I swear there's a short in the wiring in that building. \n\nAnyway... no. Not depressed. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time, even if it's just in the back of your mind?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I know you keep asking about feeling down or depressed, but it's really just... I don't know, a flatline. Just blank. \n\nSpeaking of flatlines, though, my car battery completely died on me last week. Just totally flat. I turned the key and nothing, not even a click. I had to call my neighbor, Greg, to give me a jump. Greg's a nice guy, but he talks so much. He stood there in the freezing cold for twenty minutes telling me about his son's baseball tournament. His son plays travel ball, and apparently, it costs a fortune. They have to buy all these matching uniforms, and they travel to different states every weekend. I can't imagine spending all your weekends sitting on cold aluminum bleachers eating stale sunflower seeds. \n\nI used to love sunflower seeds when I was a kid. The ranch-flavored ones. My brother and I would sit on the porch and see who could spit the shells the farthest into the yard. My mom hated it. She said it ruined the grass. She was so particular about her lawn. She used to edge the driveway with these little rusty shears every Saturday morning... \n\nBut wait, what were you asking? Oh, feeling sad. No, definitely not sad. Just... yeah, just empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. Honestly, that's what made me come in. I feel like the warm part of me just... shut off. Like, I sit with my husband after work, or I try to watch my shows, and I know I should feel comfort or even a little happy, but there's just nothing there. \n\nMy husband usually watches those history documentaries. He loves anything about World War II or ancient Rome. He was watching this one last night about the Colosseum, and how they used to flood the whole arena to have mock naval battles. Can you imagine the plumbing required for that? Just filling a whole stadium with water. It makes my leaky basement seem like a joke. \n\nAnyway, he was so into it, and he kept pausing it to tell me facts about the gladiators. Did you know they had a special diet? Mostly barley and beans. They called them \"barley men.\" Which sounds like a terrible diet. I can't imagine eating just beans all day. I tried to do a vegetarian diet once back in my twenties. I lasted about a week before I caved and bought a bacon cheeseburger at this little diner near my old apartment. The diner had those classic red vinyl booths. You know the ones that kind of stick to the back of your legs in the summer if you're wearing shorts? \n\nBut yeah... like I said, I just don't feel anything. No joy or comfort. Just that blank feeling again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I feel like we just talked about this, didn't we? You asked me about being depressed a few minutes ago. But no, really, not even a little bit. Like I keep saying, it's just that flat, empty feeling. 

Speaking of flat, though, I work at a distribution warehouse, filling orders, and yesterday one of the new guys ran over a box of these tiny little hot sauce bottles with the forklift. Just flattened the whole case completely. It was a huge mess. The whole aisle smelled like vinegar and chili peppers for the rest of my shift. It actually made my eyes water.

I have this nice leather wallet my aunt got me for Christmas a few years ago, and if hot sauce got on that, it would ruin the leather completely. You have to be so careful with real leather. You're supposed to use a special conditioner on it every few months, but who actually remembers to do that? I bought a bottle of leather conditioner once, and it's just been sitting under my kitchen sink next to the glass cleaner. 

But anyway... yeah. Not depressed. Definitely not. Just blank.

They parked over by the administrative offices, which makes absolutely no sense because there's this massive sign that says 'Shipping and Receiving' with a bright red arrow pointing around the back of the building. \n\nBut people just ignore signs all the time. I feel bad for mail carriers, honestly. They have to deal with so much, especially in the summer when it's boiling hot and those little square trucks don't even have air conditioning. My uncle used to work for the post office back in the nineties, and he said the heat in those things was just unbearable. He ended up quitting to open a hardware store, but that went out of business when Home Depot moved into town. It's a shame, really, because he used to give us a family discount on paint and stuff whenever we wanted to redecorate.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've lost any weight. I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes still fit the same. I usually get my work pants from this outlet mall a couple of towns over, and they're pretty sturdy. The last time I was there, though, the traffic on I-95 was just unbelievable. They've been doing construction on that one overpass for, I swear, three years now. \n\nAnd they always do the lane closures at the worst possible times, right in the middle of the day on a Saturday. We were sitting there bumper to bumper for an hour, and this lady in the lane next to me was putting on a full face of makeup while she was driving. Like, mascara, eyeliner, the whole thing. I don't know how people do that. If I tried that, I'd poke my eye out. My mom used to do that when she'd drive us to school, actually. She had this old Buick LeSabre with those velvet seats that smelled like vanilla air freshener and old coffee. She loved that car. She finally sold it to some kid down the street who painted it bright orange. It looks terrible now, like a giant pumpkin on wheels. \n\nBut, uh, no, my weight is about the same. I haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad, just... blank, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions. Which is kind of how I feel when I have to go to the grocery store on a Sunday afternoon. It's just a chore, you know? The aisles are always so packed, and the carts at my local store have those wonky wheels that squeak and pull to the left. I always seem to get the bad cart. \n\nAnd the last time I was there, they had rearranged the whole cereal aisle. Why do they do that? You get used to things being in one spot, and then suddenly the Cheerios are where the oatmeal used to be. It took me ten minutes just to find the brand I like. There was this lady stocking the shelves, and she looked so tired, like she'd been there since 4 AM. I totally get that look, working in a warehouse. You just hit a wall. But anyway, no, I wouldn't say I feel sad all day. Just that weird blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Um, no. Not depressed, really. Just that same empty feeling I was talking about. It's weird, it's kind of like... you know when you walk into a huge room and it's completely cleared out? Like the warehouse on a holiday weekend when the skeleton crew is on. It's so quiet you can actually hear those big overhead fluorescent lights buzzing. \n\nI hate that sound. They've been saying they're going to upgrade us to LEDs for, like, two years now. The maintenance guy, Gary, he says the parts are stuck on a cargo ship somewhere. Which is what everyone blames everything on these days, right? Everything is always stuck on a ship. I actually saw a documentary about those cargo ships on TV the other night. They are absolutely massive. The people who work on them have to be out at sea for months at a time, just surrounded by water. I could never do that. Just looking at the ocean makes me feel a little seasick. My parents took us on a cruise to the Bahamas when I was in middle school, and I spent the first three days just lying in the cabin eating saltines and watching old movies on this tiny little TV they had bolted to the wall. The cabin was so small, too, like a closet with bunk beds. My brother kept kicking the bottom of my mattress the whole time. \n\nBut anyway, what were we... oh, depressed. No, I don't think so. Just numb.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Interest in things? Um... I don't really know. I guess I haven't been doing much of anything lately outside of work. I used to go to this bowling league on Thursday nights with some people from the warehouse, but I just haven't felt like going. Bowling is getting so expensive anyway. The alley near my house just raised their prices for shoe rentals, and they don't even clean them properly. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. It's funny how places always ruin a good thing to save a few bucks. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. I tried complaining to the barista, but it wasn't her fault. She's this sweet college kid who's always studying for biology exams behind the counter. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. Like I said, it's really just that blank feeling. But speaking of \"a little bit,\" my supervisor at the warehouse, Dave, he's always walking around telling us to just give \"a little bit more\" on our pick rates. Like, how much more can we give? We're already scanning hundreds of boxes an hour. \n\nAnd those handheld scanners we use are practically ancient. The batteries die halfway through a shift, and you have to walk all the way across the concrete floor to the charging station. Which is right next to the breakroom. And I swear, the breakroom always smells like someone burnt popcorn. Why do people always burn popcorn in the microwave? It's like they hit the five-minute button and just walk away. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend. You'd think they'd put a sign up or something, but Dave says we don't have the budget for \"non-essential signage.\" Whatever that means. \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, being depressed. Yeah, no, I don't think I'm depressed. Just that weird empty feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Low mood? No, not really. I mean, I'm not sad or down or anything like that. Just... blank. Like nothing's really registering. But speaking of registering, the new cash registers they put in at the grocery store down the street are so confusing. They replaced all the regular lanes with these giant self-checkout machines that talk to you. \n\nThe voice is so loud, too. \"Please place your item in the bagging area.\" Like, I did! I put the apples right there, and it still flashes that red light and makes you wait for the attendant. The attendant is usually this older lady, Brenda, I think her name tag says. She always looks so tired. I don't blame her, having to run back and forth swiping her card every time someone buys a cucumber. She told me last week her feet were killing her because she bought these new shoes online and they didn't fit right. Buying shoes online is such a gamble. You never know if the sizing is going to be weird, and then returning them is a huge hassle. You have to print the label, find a box, tape it all up, and then drive all the way to the post office. The line at our post office is always out the door, especially around the holidays. People shipping all these giant boxes to their grandkids... \n\nWait, what was the question? Low mood? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say my mood is low. Just... not really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood, even if it's just some of the time?\n\nPatient: Depressed mood? No, really, I'm not depressed. Like I said, it's just this... nothingness. Just blank. \n\nBut you know what is depressing? The weather we've been having. It's been raining for, like, three days straight. My basement actually started leaking yesterday. Just a little puddle near the water heater, but still. I had to call my landlord, Mr. Henderson, and he's always so slow to fix things. Last year, the radiator in my bedroom was making this awful clanking noise, like someone hitting a pipe with a wrench. Clank. Clank. All night long. I called him four times before he finally sent someone out. \n\nAnd the guy he sent was this plumber who tracked mud all over my rug. It's this nice braided rug I found at a flea market a few summers ago. I love flea markets. You can find the most interesting stuff. I bought this old, vintage metal lunchbox there once—it had the Jetsons on it. I don't even use it for lunch, I just keep spare batteries and lightbulbs in it. Which is handy, because the lightbulb in my hallway keeps flickering. I swear there's a short in the wiring in that building. \n\nAnyway... no. Not depressed. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time, even if it's just in the back of your mind?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. I know you keep asking about feeling down or depressed, but it's really just... I don't know, a flatline. Just blank. \n\nSpeaking of flatlines, though, my car battery completely died on me last week. Just totally flat. I turned the key and nothing, not even a click. I had to call my neighbor, Greg, to give me a jump. Greg's a nice guy, but he talks so much. He stood there in the freezing cold for twenty minutes telling me about his son's baseball tournament. His son plays travel ball, and apparently, it costs a fortune. They have to buy all these matching uniforms, and they travel to different states every weekend. I can't imagine spending all your weekends sitting on cold aluminum bleachers eating stale sunflower seeds. \n\nI used to love sunflower seeds when I was a kid. The ranch-flavored ones. My brother and I would sit on the porch and see who could spit the shells the farthest into the yard. My mom hated it. She said it ruined the grass. She was so particular about her lawn. She used to edge the driveway with these little rusty shears every Saturday morning... \n\nBut wait, what were you asking? Oh, feeling sad. No, definitely not sad. Just... yeah, just empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you joy?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. Honestly, that's what made me come in. I feel like the warm part of me just... shut off. Like, I sit with my husband after work, or I try to watch my shows, and I know I should feel comfort or even a little happy, but there's just nothing there. \n\nMy husband usually watches those history documentaries. He loves anything about World War II or ancient Rome. He was watching this one last night about the Colosseum, and how they used to flood the whole arena to have mock naval battles. Can you imagine the plumbing required for that? Just filling a whole stadium with water. It makes my leaky basement seem like a joke. \n\nAnyway, he was so into it, and he kept pausing it to tell me facts about the gladiators. Did you know they had a special diet? Mostly barley and beans. They called them \"barley men.\" Which sounds like a terrible diet. I can't imagine eating just beans all day. I tried to do a vegetarian diet once back in my twenties. I lasted about a week before I caved and bought a bacon cheeseburger at this little diner near my old apartment. The diner had those classic red vinyl booths. You know the ones that kind of stick to the back of your legs in the summer if you're wearing shorts? \n\nBut yeah... like I said, I just don't feel anything. No joy or comfort. Just that blank feeling again.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are depressed, even if it's just a little?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just talked about this, didn't we? Just flattened the whole case completely. It was a huge mess. The whole aisle smelled like vinegar and chili peppers for the rest of my shift. It actually made my eyes water. \n\nIt smelled exactly like this taco truck that used to park near my high school. My friend Sarah and I would get these spicy pork tacos every Friday. Sarah was obsessed with hot sauce. She actually carried a bottle of it in her purse. Which is dangerous, if you think about it. Imagine that opening up in your bag? Getting all over your wallet and your keys and everything. I have this nice leather wallet my aunt got me for Christmas a few years ago, and if hot sauce got on that, it would ruin the leather completely. You have to be so careful with real leather. Just blank.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Clank. Clank. All night long. I called him four times before he finally sent someone out. \n\nAnd the guy he sent was this plumber who tracked mud all over my rug. It's this nice braided rug I found at a flea market a few summers ago. I love flea markets. You can find the most interesting stuff. I bought this old, vintage metal lunchbox there once—it had the Jetsons on it. I don't even use it for lunch, I just keep spare batteries and lightbulbs in it. Which is handy, because the lightbulb in my hallway keeps flickering. I swear there's a short in the wiring in that building. \n\nAnyway... no. Not depressed. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time, even if it's just in the back of your mind?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I wouldn't say sad. His son plays travel ball, and apparently, it costs a fortune. They have to buy all these matching uniforms, and they travel to different states every weekend. I can't imagine spending all your weekends sitting on cold aluminum bleachers eating stale sunflower seeds. \n\nI used to love sunflower seeds when I was a kid. The ranch-flavored ones. Low mood? I mean, I really feel like we're going in circles here. Are you asking if I'm sad again? Because I really don't think I am. It's just that same blankness I keep telling you about.

I get home from all that, and I sit with my husband, or I try to watch my regular shows, and I know I should feel comfort, or even a little happy just to be off my feet and home, but... there’s just nothing there. I don't feel anything good.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice this feeling of emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: It's been... about three weeks now. Yeah, exactly three weeks. It started right after this horrible thing happened at the warehouse—a pallet collapsed and really badly injured one of my coworkers. Which, honestly, I am not even surprised it happened, because I've been telling Dave for months that the shrink wrap they buy now is super cheap and it doesn't secure the loads right. Plus, they always stick the heaviest stuff, like those giant bulk boxes of industrial detergent, way too high up on the racks. \n\nMy husband does construction, and he always says you never put the heavy stuff up high like that. He actually threw his back out a couple years ago because some new guy stacked bags of cement wrong, and dealing with worker's comp for that was just... a total nightmare. I mean, they fought us on every single physical therapy bill, and we were on the phone for hours every week just trying to get someone to approve his medication. It was so stressful. But, um... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, ever since that pallet fell on my coworker three weeks ago, I just... I haven't felt anything. Just that blank feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before these three weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really. Like I was saying a minute ago, it's like that warm part of me is just completely gone. I used to really love watching those baking competition shows to unwind. My sister actually got me into them a few years ago. She bakes all the time—she even made this massive three-tier cake for my niece's sweet sixteen last month. It was crazy, it took her like three whole days to finish it. The frosting kept melting, though, because the AC in her apartment has been broken since July, and her landlord is completely useless. He keeps saying he's going to send a maintenance guy out, but it's always \"next Tuesday, next Tuesday.\" It's ridiculous how much rent she pays just to sweat in her own kitchen, honestly. She's thinking about breaking her lease. \n\nBut, um... yeah, anyway. I used to love putting those shows on. Now when I sit down on the couch with my husband to watch them, I know I should be enjoying it, or feeling cozy and happy to be home, but... I just don't. I don't feel anything good at all.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasurable or enjoyable, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I wish I did. But like I was saying, it's just... I feel like the warm part of me completely shut off. I get home, I sit with my husband, I try to watch my shows, and I know I should feel comfort, or even just a little happy to be off my feet, but there's just... nothing there. \n\nMy husband tries, you know? He brought home takeout from that Thai place we usually love on Friday. The one on 4th Street? They have the best Pad Thai, though the last time we went, they completely forgot the peanuts, which is like the best part. And the parking around there is just impossible now since they put in those new bike lanes. I don't even know who uses those bike lanes anyway, I never see anyone on them, but they took away half the street parking for it. We had to park like three blocks away in the rain once. \n\nBut yeah... he brought the food home, and we sat down, and I just... I couldn't even enjoy it. I didn't feel anything. Just empty.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge since this emotional numbness started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have been. I mean, especially at work. Whenever I'm anywhere near receiving now, I'm just constantly checking everything. Like, I keep checking the racks, looking at the pallet height, scanning to see where the exits are, and I'm always looking over my shoulder to see who's moving behind me. It's like I can't trust the floor to be safe for even a minute. \n\nWhich is exhausting, honestly, because our receiving area is huge. It's the size of a football field. They actually expanded it two years ago when they bought out the lot next door. That used to be this old diner, Denny's or something, I can't remember, but they tore it down. It was kind of sad, actually, because my husband and I used to get breakfast there sometimes when we first started dating. They had these incredible blueberry pancakes. But the service was always so slow. One time we waited like forty-five minutes just for coffee, and the waitress was so rude about it when we finally asked. I get being short-staffed, but you don't have to take it out on the customers, you know? \n\nBut, um... yeah. I just feel like I'm always on edge now, constantly scanning the warehouse. I just can't relax.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I feel empty, but I don't want to die or anything. I just want to feel normal again. \n\nMy cousin actually went through a really dark period a few years ago where she was talking like that. It was really scary for the whole family. She had just gone through this awful divorce. Her husband left her for his personal trainer, which is just such a cliché, right? And the trainer was like twenty-two or something ridiculous. They met at that gym on 5th Avenue, the one with the huge glass windows where everyone can see you working out while they're waiting for the bus. I never understood why people want to pay so much money just to sweat in front of traffic. I tried a membership there once, but they charge you for everything. Like, you even have to bring your own towel or they charge you three dollars to rent one. It's such a scam. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, she was in a really bad place, but she got help and she's doing much better now. She actually just started dating a guy who works in IT. But for me... no, I haven't had any thoughts about hurting myself or anything like that. I just want this empty feeling to go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest in most activities or a decrease in pleasure for most of the day, every day, since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's like, every single minute of every day. I still do my normal routine, you know? Like, I still get up and drive to the warehouse every morning. Though honestly, I might need to take my car into the shop soon because it's been making this awful rattling noise whenever I go over forty miles an hour. It's a Honda Civic, and usually, it runs great, but my brother-in-law borrowed it a few weeks ago to pick up some lumber for a deck he's building, and it hasn't sounded right since. \n\nHe's always doing stuff like that, too. Just borrowing things and bringing them back messed up. He borrowed my husband's power washer last spring and returned it without the nozzle. How do you even lose the nozzle? Oh, about losing interest in things every day. Yeah, I don't know if it's all day, every day. It's mostly just that weird numb feeling I mentioned before when I'm at home trying to relax.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't enjoy the things you used to?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been about three weeks. Yeah, exactly three weeks, ever since the pallet collapsed and injured my coworker. It was just... a huge mess. The ambulance actually took forever to get to our loading dock because the driver went to the wrong entrance. They parked over by the administrative offices, which makes absolutely no sense because there's this massive sign that says 'Shipping and Receiving' with a bright red arrow pointing around the back of the building. \n\nBut people just ignore signs all the time. Like the stop sign at the end of my street. People just blow right through it every single day. Just yesterday, this guy in a silver pickup truck almost T-boned a mail truck because he didn't even tap his brakes. The mail carrier was so mad, he was laying on his horn and yelling out the window. I feel bad for mail carriers, honestly. They have to deal with so much, especially in the summer when it's boiling hot and those little square trucks don't even have air conditioning. My uncle used to work for the post office back in the nineties, and he said the heat in those things was just unbearable. He ended up quitting to open a hardware store, but that went out of business when Home Depot moved into town. It's a shame, really, because he used to give us a family discount on paint and stuff whenever we wanted to redecorate.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this emotional numbness?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've lost any weight. I haven't really been checking the scale or anything, but my clothes still fit the same. I usually get my work pants from this outlet mall a couple of towns over, and they're pretty sturdy. The last time I was there, though, the traffic on I-95 was just unbelievable. They've been doing construction on that one overpass for, I swear, three years now. \n\nAnd they always do the lane closures at the worst possible times, right in the middle of the day on a Saturday. We were sitting there bumper to bumper for an hour, and this lady in the lane next to me was putting on a full face of makeup while she was driving. Like, mascara, eyeliner, the whole thing. I don't know how people do that. If I tried that, I'd poke my eye out. My mom used to do that when she'd drive us to school, actually. She had this old Buick LeSabre with those velvet seats that smelled like vanilla air freshener and old coffee. She loved that car. She finally sold it to some kid down the street who painted it bright orange. It looks terrible now, like a giant pumpkin on wheels. \n\nBut, uh, no, my weight is about the same. I haven't noticed any difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel sad, just... blank, I guess. Like I'm just going through the motions. Which is kind of how I feel when I have to go to the grocery store on a Sunday afternoon. It's just a chore, you know? The aisles are always so packed, and the carts at my local store have those wonky wheels that squeak and pull to the left. I always seem to get the bad cart. \n\nAnd the last time I was there, they had rearranged the whole cereal aisle. Why do they do that? You get used to things being in one spot, and then suddenly the Cheerios are where the oatmeal used to be. It took me ten minutes just to find the brand I like. There was this lady stocking the shelves, and she looked so tired, like she'd been there since 4 AM. I totally get that look, working in a warehouse. You just hit a wall. Like the warehouse on a holiday weekend when the skeleton crew is on. It's so quiet you can actually hear those big overhead fluorescent lights buzzing. \n\nI hate that sound. They are absolutely massive. The people who work on them have to be out at sea for months at a time, just surrounded by water. I could never do that. Just looking at the ocean makes me feel a little seasick. My parents took us on a cruise to the Bahamas when I was in middle school, and I spent the first three days just lying in the cabin eating saltines and watching old movies on this tiny little TV they had bolted to the wall. Just numb.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Interest in things? Um... I don't really know. The last time I went, the shoes were still warm from the person before me. It's so gross. \n\nAnd the food there is terrible now, too. They used to have these amazing chili cheese fries, but they changed the cheese sauce to this weird, watery stuff that tastes like plastic. Like my favorite coffee shop downtown—they stopped carrying the almond milk I like and switched to this generic brand that separates in the cup. It looks like curdled milk. She told me she wants to be a marine biologist, which sounds really cool, but also a lot of pressure. I don't know how kids afford college these days anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really been doing much. I just haven't noticed if I've lost interest or not, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not even a little bit. The smell lingers for days, and it gets into your clothes. My jacket smelled like burnt butter all weekend.
